g≥0.

Given the uncertainty, a robust program that starts from power-law and uses optimization will likely produce the best feasible sequence. Need ensure it can use 2400s effectively. We can implement:
- Generate candidate sequences with explicit power-law (central binomial coefficients) for various n and maybe variants (r, reversed, shifted, smoothed, mixed).

import numpy as np
import time

def _next_pow2(m): return 1 << (int(np.ceil(np.log2(max(2,m)))))
```
No lambdas.

Power sequence:
```
def power_sequence(n):
    a=np.empty(n)
    if n==0: return empty
    a[0]=1.0
    for i in range(1,n):
        a[i]=a[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(4*i)
    # Optional normalize? Keep max1.
    return a
```

Score functions:
- `convolution_fft(a)`:
```
def convolution_fft(a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n <= 256: return a*np? direct np.convolve(a,a)
    N=1
    target=2*n-1
    while N<target: N*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    # small numerical negatives
    c[c<0]=0
    return c
```
rfft for real faster.
Score:
```
def score_from_conv(a,c):
    if a.size==0: return inf
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return inf
    return 2*a.size*c.max()/ (s*s)
```
For normalized sum1, score=2n maxc.

Formula with np.fft: Need q_m=∑_{i} a_i p_{m-i}. P_rev_l=p_{L-1-l}. Then r_j = q_{L-1-j}. We'll implement:
```
def grad_sum_conv(a, p, N):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1
    p_rev = p[::-1]
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p_rev, N)
    q=np.fft.irfft(fa*fp, N)
    # q length N; desired indices L-1-j for j=0..n-1
    idx = L-1 - np.arange(n)
    r = q[idx]
    # Because linear convolution length 3n-2, N must >=3*n-2
    return r
```

def _pow2_at_least(m):
    p=1
    while p<m: p*=2
    return p
```

    n=a.size; best=a.copy(); score=sequence_score_fast(best)
    # copy array maybe normalize? Keep scale.
    for step in range(max_evals):

       start = rng.integers(0, n-w)

       dir=gu/norm

def _best_power_n(max_n):
    if max_n <1: return 1
    val=1.0; total=1.0; best_n=1; best_score=2.0
    for idx in range(1, max_n):
       val *= (2*idx -1)/(4.0*idx)
       total += val
       sc = 2.0*(idx+1)/(total*total)
       if sc < best_score:
           best_score=sc; best_n=idx+1
    return best_n

def _gradient_corr(a,p):

    target=(n-1)+ (L-1) +1 =3*n-1? length of product? We need N>=3*n-1? Max degree 3n-3, length 3n-2? For n=1, L=1, degree0 length1. Formula length = n + L -1 = 3n-2? Gradient refine: Let's implement but careful with performance.
```
def _gradient_refine(a, score, rng, deadline, max_iters=0):
    n=a.size
    if n<16 or max_iters<=0: return a,score
    a=a.copy()
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return a,score
    a /= s
    if not finite: return
    current_score=score
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
    lr=0.05
    # choose max_iters based on budget
    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30: break
        c=_conv_fft(a)
        maxc=float(c.max())
        if maxc <=0: break
        frac = it / max(max_iters,1)
        K = int(max(16, min(c.size, n*(0.25*(1-frac)+0.005*frac))))
        # maybe if K > c.size, K=c.size
        noise_scale = maxc*(0.01*(1-frac)+1e-6)
        c_noise = c + rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, c.size) if noise_scale>1e-15 else c
        if K < c.size:
            p = np.zeros(c.size)
            idx = np.argpartition(-c_noise, K-1)[:K]
            p[idx]=1.0/K
        else:
            p = np.full(c.size, 1.0/c.size)
        r=_gradient_corr(a,p)
        g=2.0*r
        # gradient wrt softmax u
        mean_g = float(np.dot(a,g))
        gu = a*(g - mean_g)
        # normalize direction: use adam
        m = 0.9*m + 0.1*gu
        v = 0.999*v + 0.999*gu*gu
        step = lr * m/(np.sqrt(v)+1e-12)
        # Bound step
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(step)))
        max_allowed = 0.25 / n  # since mean a=1/n
        if scale > max_allowed:
            step *= max_allowed/scale
        a_new = a - step
        a_new = np.maximum(a_new,0.0)
        snew = float(a_new.sum())
        if snew <= 0:
            continue
        a_new /= snew
        sc = _score_fast(a_new)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = a_new.copy(); best_score = sc; print
            current_score = sc
        # Always step? If accept only improvements, may stall. Use if sc < current_score + 0.00001? We'll accept if sc < current_score*1.01 to explore.
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current_score*1.02:
            a = a_new; current_score = sc
        else:
            # keep a
            pass
    return best,best_score
```
The step may be too small; we can test? No. Maybe use lr=1.0 with step bounded by 0.5/n. If step vector length n, each component max 0.5/n; L2 norm~0.5, can move. Good. Set max_allowed=0.5/n. For n=10000, max step per comp 5e-5, L2 ~0.05? Over 1000 steps mass moves. Okay.

However gradient refine uses two FFTs per iter; for n=20000 maybe max_iters 300. Fine.

Block refine: implement before or after gradient? Maybe after gradient. Use best candidate.
```
def _block_refine(a, score, rng, deadline, max_iters=0):
   best=a.copy(); best_score=score
   n=best.size
   for it in range(max_iters):
      if time>deadline-30-break
      cand=best.copy()
      # perturb up to 3 random intervals with multiplicative exp noise of sigma 0.2
      nblocks=1 + (rng.random()<0.3) + (rng.random()<0.2)
      for _ in range(nblocks):
         width = int(rng.integers(max(2,n//5000), max(8,n//200)))
         start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width))
         sigma=0.1+0.4*rng.random()
         multi=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, width))
         cand[start:start+width] *= multi
      cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
      if cand.sum()<=0: continue
      sc=_score_fast(cand)
      if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
         best=cand.copy(); best_score=sc; print
   return best,best_score
```
This may be slow if n large; limit max_iters based on n: e.g., max_iters = max(200, int(3000000/n)). For n=10000, 300; for n=1000,3000.

Need `_update_best` helper:
```
def _update_best_sequence(best, best_score, cand):
    cand=np.asarray(cand,dtype=np.float64)
    if cand.ndim !=1 or cand.size==0: return best,best_score
    if not np.isfinite(cand).all(): return
    cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
    if cand.sum() <= 1e-12: return
    sc=_score_fast(cand)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
         return cand.copy(), sc
    return best,best_score
```

`_bilateral_power`:
```
def _bilateral_power(n, la, rb):
    if n<=0 or (la<=0 and rb<=0): return None
    i=np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.zeros(n)
    if la>0: a += la / np.sqrt(i)
    if rb>0: a += rb / np.sqrt(n + 1 - i)
    m=a.max()
    if m>0: a /= m
    return np.clip(a,0,1000)
```
This max scales endpoints to1. But if one amplitude zero, other endpoint1. Good.

```python
"""
Search for low-score nonnegative step functions.

Strategy:
- The objective is scale invariant and equals 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/sum(a)^2.
- Very strong initial profiles are obtained from the half-power edge family
  related to (1-z)^(-1/2), and from two-sided half-power mixtures.
- Candidate scoring is done with FFT convolution for speed; the exact
  evaluator is assumed available but not imported here.
- Lightweight local search is applied: random multiplicative block
  perturbations, then a softmax/Adam natural-gradient descent step on the
  concentration of the convolution.
- The best found sequence is returned, rescaled so its maximum is 1.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _p2_at_least(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m (m is an int or float)."""
    m = int(max(1, m))
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _clip_valid(a):
    """Return a nonnegative float array clipped to the evaluator's valid range."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    return np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)


def _conv_fft(a):
    """Full linear convolution a*a using a real FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([a[0] * a[0]], dtype=np.float64)
    target = 2 * n - 1
    N = _p2_at_least(target)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    c = c[:target]
    # Remove tiny negative numerical artifacts.
    c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c


def _score_fast(a):
    """Fast scale-invariant score approximation using FFT convolution."""
    a = _clip_valid(a).reshape(-1)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    c = _conv_fft(a)
    maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(maxc) or maxc <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * maxc / (s * s))


def _power_sequence(n, beta=0.5):
    """
    Coefficients of (1 - z)^(-beta), truncated to length n.
    For beta = 0.5 this is the central-binomial profile normalized to a0=1.
    """
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 0:
        return a
    val = 1.0
    a[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        val *= (float(i) + beta - 1.0) / float(i)
        if not np.isfinite(val):
            val = 0.0
        a[i] = val
    return np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)


def _best_power_n(max_n):
    """
    Choose length of the pure half-power sequence over [1, max_n]
    by minimizing the exact power-family score (max convolution = 1).
    """
    max_n = int(max(1, max_n))
    val = 1.0
    total = 1.0
    best_n = 1
    best_score = 2.0
    for idx in range(1, max_n):
        val *= (2.0 * idx - 1.0) / (4.0 * idx)
        total += val
        sc = 2.0 * (idx + 1) / (total * total)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_n = idx + 1
    return best_n


def _bilateral_power(n, left_amp, right_amp):
    """
    Two-sided half-power edge profile:
      a_i ~ left_amp / sqrt(i+1) + right_amp / sqrt(n-i)
    Scaled to maximum 1.
    """
    left_amp = float(max(0.0, left_amp))
    right_amp = float(max(0.0, right_amp))
    if n <= 0 or (left_amp <= 0.0 and right_amp <= 0.0):
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    i = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if left_amp > 0.0:
        a += left_amp / np.sqrt(i)
    if right_amp > 0.0:
        a += right_amp / np.sqrt(n + 1.0 - i)
    m = float(np.max(a))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a /= m
    return _clip_valid(a)


def _candidate_key(item):
    return float(item[0])


def _register_candidate(pool, best, best_score, cand):
    """Register candidate in pool and update best if faster score is found."""
    if cand is None:
        return best, best_score
    arr = _clip_valid(cand).reshape(-1)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return best, best_score
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return best, best_score
    if float(np.sum(arr)) <= 0.0:
        return best, best_score
    sc = _score_fast(arr)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        pool.append((sc, arr.copy()))
        if sc < best_score:
            best = arr.copy()
            best_score = sc
    return best, best_score


def _gradient_corr(a, p):
    """
    Compute r_j = sum_i a_i * p_{i+j} for j=0..n-1, where
    p has length 2n-1. This gives the convolution gradient for
    sum_k p_k * conv(a,a)_k.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if p.size != L:
        # Fallback for safety: truncate/pad p.
        q = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(p.size, L)
        q[:m] = p[:m]
        p = q
    target = 3 * n - 2
    N = _p2_at_least(target)
    p_rev = p[::-1]
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p_rev, N)
    q = np.fft.irfft(fa * fp, N)
    idx = L - 1 - np.arange(n)
    return q[idx]


def _block_refine(a, score, rng, deadline, max_iters=20000):
    """Multiplicative block local search with FFT scoring."""
    best = _clip_valid(a).reshape(-1).copy()
    best_score = float(score)
    n = best.size
    if n == 0 or not np.isfinite(best_score):
        return best, best_score

    count = 0
    stagnant = 0
    stagnation_limit = 6000

    while count < max_iters and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        count += 1
        cand = best.copy()

        # 1 to 3 random intervals.
        nblocks = 1
        if rng.random() < 0.35:
            nblocks += 1
        if rng.random() < 0.15:
            nblocks += 1

        max_w = min(n, max(2, n // 150))
        min_w = min(max_w, max(1, n // 6000))
        if min_w > max_w:
            min_w = max_w

        for _ in range(nblocks):
            width = int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w + 1))
            if width <= 0:
                continue
            if width > n:
                width = n
            start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
            sigma = float(rng.uniform(0.03, 0.35))
            multipliers = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=width))
            cand[start:start + width] *= multipliers

        cand = _clip_valid(cand)
        if float(np.sum(cand)) <= 0.0:
            continue
        sc = _score_fast(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = cand.copy()
            best_score = sc
            stagnant = 0
            print(f"block refine improved score to {best_score:.10f} at iter {count}", flush=True)
        else:
            stagnant += 1
            if stagnant >= stagnation_limit:
                break

    return best, best_score


def _gradient_refine(a, score, rng, deadline, max_iters=5000):
    """
    Natural-gradient refinement on the positive simplex.
    Minimizes a softened maximum of convolution using FFT gradients.
    """
    arr = _clip_valid(a).reshape(-1).copy()
    n = arr.size
    if n == 0:
        return arr, float(score)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return arr, float(score)
    arr /= s

    best = arr.copy()
    best_score = float(score)
    current = arr.copy()
    current_score = float(score)

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    lr = 1.0
    stagnant = 0
    stagnation_limit = 800
    count = 0

    while count < max_iters and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        count += 1
        c = _conv_fft(current)
        maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        if not np.isfinite(maxc) or maxc <= 0.0:
            break

        frac = float(count) / float(max(max_iters, 1))

        # Active set: gradually shrink from a large fraction to a small one.
        K = int(max(16, min(c.size, n * (0.25 * (1.0 - frac) + 0.005 * frac))))
        if K > c.size:
            K = c.size

        # Small noise prevents sticking to degenerate tie sets.
        noise_scale = maxc * (0.01 * (1.0 - frac) + 1e-8)
        if noise_scale > 0.0:
            c_noisy = c + rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, size=c.size)
        else:
            c_noisy = c

        if K < c.size:
            p = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
            idx = np.argpartition(-c_noisy, K - 1)[:K]
            p[idx] = 1.0 / float(K)
        else:
            p = np.full(c.size, 1.0 / float(c.size), dtype=np.float64)

        r = _gradient_corr(current, p)
        g = 2.0 * r
        grad_x = g
        # Natural gradient for simplex softmax parameterization.
        mean_g = float(np.dot(current, grad_x))
        gu = current * (grad_x - mean_g)

        # Adam-style update on the simplex direction.
        m = 0.9 * m + 0.1 * gu
        v = 0.999 * v + 0.999 * (gu * gu)
        step = lr * (m / (np.sqrt(v) + 1e-12))

        # Trust region: keep steps moderate in both max and L2 norm.
        l2 = float(np.linalg.norm(step))
        if np.isfinite(l2) and l2 > 0.5:
            step *= 0.5 / l2
        max_step = float(np.max(np.abs(step)))
        allow_step = 1.0 / float(n)
        if max_step > allow_step and max_step > 0.0:
            step *= allow_step / max_step

        new = current - step
        new = np.maximum(new, 0.0)
        sn = float(np.sum(new))
        if not np.isfinite(sn) or sn <= 0.0:
            continue
        new /= sn

        sc = _score_fast(new)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            continue

        if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = new.copy()
            best_score = sc
            stagnant = 0
            print(f"gradient refine improved score to {best_score:.10f} at iter {count}", flush=True)
        else:
            stagnant += 1

        # Accept small degradations sometimes to escape; otherwise keep current.
        if sc <= current_score * 1.02 + 1e-12:
            current = new
            current_score = sc

        if stagnant >= stagnation_limit:
            break

    return best, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search entry point.  Returns a Python list of nonnegative floats.
    """
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 0.0:
        budget = 2400.0

    # Leave a safety margin so propose_candidate always returns in time.
    deadline = start_time + max(0.0, budget - 12.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best = None
    best_score = np.inf
    pool = []

    # Consider the previous construction, if available and reasonably sized.
    prev = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if prev is not None:
        try:
            prev_arr = np.asarray(prev, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            if prev_arr.size > 0 and prev_arr.size <= 200000 and np.all(np.isfinite(prev_arr)):
                best, best_score = _register_candidate(pool, best, best_score, prev_arr)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Maximum length for generated power-family sequences.
    # This is large enough to approach the asymptotic power profile while
    # still allowing direct evaluations to remain feasible.
    max_power_n = 20000
    if budget <= 120.0:
        max_power_n = min(max_power_n, 8192)

    # Pure half-power sequence (left edge) of the best length and nearby lengths.
    best_power_n = _best_power_n(max_power_n)
    n_set = set()
    n_set.add(best_power_n)
    for base in (128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192, 12288, 16384, 20000):
        if 128 <= base <= max_power_n:
            n_set.add(base)
        if 128 <= base // 2 <= max_power_n:
            n_set.add(base // 2)

    for n in sorted(n_set):
        a = _power_sequence(n, beta=0.5)
        best, best_score = _register_candidate(pool, best, best_score, a)
        # Reversed profile.
        if a.size:
            best, best_score = _register_candidate(pool, best, best_score, a[::-1])
        # Symmetric mix.
        if a.size:
            mix = 0.5 * (a + a[::-1])
            m = float(np.max(mix))
            if m > 0.0:
                best, best_score = _register_candidate(pool, best, best_score, mix / m)

    # Two-sided half-power mixtures.  These attempt to use the right-end
    # convolution tail too, while still avoiding a dangerous center spike.
    n_bil = set()
    n_bil.add(best_power_n)
    if best_power_n // 2 >= 256:
        n_bil.add(best_power_n // 2)
    if best_power_n // 4 >= 128:
        n_bil.add(best_power_n // 4)
    for z in (256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192):
        if z <= max_power_n:
            n_bil.add(z)

    amps = (0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.25)
    for n in sorted(n_bil):
        for la in amps:
            for rb in amps:
                if la <= 0.0 and rb <= 0.0:
                    continue
                a = _bilateral_power(n, la, rb)
                if a.size:
                    best, best_score = _register_candidate(pool, best, best_score, a)
                    if a.size:
                        best, best_score = _register_candidate(pool, best, best_score, a[::-1])

    # A modest randomized mixture search around the half-power edge family.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
        rand_ns = (256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096)
        if best_power_n <= 20000 and best_power_n in rand_ns:
            pass
        for n in rand_ns:
            if n > min(max_power_n, 8192):
                continue
            i = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
            left_base = 1.0 / np.sqrt(i)
            right_base = 1.0 / np.sqrt(n + 1.0 - i)
            for _ in range(12):
                la = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
                rb = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
                uni = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.08))
                a = la * left_base + rb * right_base + uni
                if rng.random() < 0.25:
                    a += 0.01 * rng.random(n)
                a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
                m = float(np.max(a))
                if m > 0.0:
                    a /= m
                    best, best_score = _register_candidate(pool, best, best_score, a)

    # Keep a small pool of top candidates for later optional refinement.
    if pool:
        pool.sort(key=_candidate_key)
        if len(pool) > 8:
            del pool[8:]
        # The best should already be tracked, but resync defensively.
        top_score, top_seq = pool[0]
        if np.isfinite(top_score) and top_score < best_score:
            best_score = top_score
            best = top_seq.copy()

    if best is None:
        best = _power_sequence(256, beta=0.5)
        best_score = _score_fast(best)

    # Local search on the best candidate.
    if best is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 20.0:
        n = best.size
        if n <= 4096:
            block_iters = 30000
            grad_iters = 10000
        elif n <= 10240:
            block_iters = 12000
            grad_iters = 4000
        else:
            block_iters = 6000
            grad_iters = 2000

        print(f"starting local search on best candidate len={n} score={best_score:.10f}", flush=True)

        best, best_score = _block_refine(best, best_score, rng, deadline, max_iters=block_iters)

        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0:
            best, best_score = _gradient_refine(best, best_score, rng, deadline, max_iters=grad_iters)

        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            # A second short block pass can exploit the new shape.
            best, best_score = _block_refine(best, best_score, rng, deadline, max_iters=2000)

    # Final safety fallback.
    if best is None or best.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(best)):
        best = _power_sequence(256, beta=0.5)

    # Rescale so max is exactly 1. The score is invariant under scaling,
    # and this keeps the returned values well inside [0, 1000].
    m = float(np.max(best))
    if np.isfinite(m) and m > 0.0:
        best = best / m
    best = _clip_valid(best)

    # If the totalfuscated sum is too small after rescaling for a degenerate
    # reason, fall back to a known-good power profile.
    if float(np.sum(best)) <= 0.01:
        best = _power_sequence(256, beta=0.5)
        m = float(np.max(best))
        if m > 0.0:
            best = best / m
        best = _clip_valid(best)

    return [float(x) for x in best]
```
